\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
satisfy the properties in Proposition
3.7
.
Then
\mathfrak{Z}
can be effectively written as a finite intersection
\bigcap_{j}\mathfrak{Z}_{j}
, where each
\mathfrak{Z}_{j}
is the solution set of an S-unit equation over some
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{V}
, satisfying

(i)

\mathcal{A}
is local, its maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
satisfies
\mathfrak{m}^{t}=0
for some
t\geq 1
.

(ii)

\mathcal{A}
is effectively represented as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

(iii)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module,
\mathcal{A}
is finitely generated.

(iv)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module,
\mathcal{V}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
In particular, every element in
\mathcal{A}
acts as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformation on
\mathcal{V}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
.

Step 1: decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module.

Recall that
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
is a principal ideal ring (PIR) whose only ideals are
\left\langle{1}\right\rangle,\left\langle{p}\right\rangle,\left\langle{p^{2}}\right\rangle
,
\ldots
,
\left\langle{p^{e-1}}\right\rangle,\left\langle{p^{e}}\right\rangle=\{0\}
.
This gives us a characterization of the structure of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module:

Lemma 3.17

(Structure theorem of finitely generated module over a PIR
[
Bro93
, Theorem 15.33]
)
.

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module,
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
can be effectively decomposed as a direct sum

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{1}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{2}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{2}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{e}}

(3.22)

for some
d_{1},\ldots,d_{e}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Here, the
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),i=1,\ldots,e
, is equal to the quotient
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)/p^{i}\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
.

If the action of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
stabilizes each component
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
in the decomposition of the
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
, then Equation (
3.22
) is also a decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as an
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module.
In this case, Proposition
3.16
easily follows by taking
\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
.
However, in general
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
does not stabilize each
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
, that is,
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}\not\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
.
Therefore Equation (
3.22
) is not a decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as an
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module.
The main idea of this subsection is to find a different decomposition of the
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
by changing the “basis”, so that the components of the new decomposition
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{1}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{2}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{2}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{e}}
are stabilized by
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
.
To achieve this, we will exploit property (iv) of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
from Proposition
3.7
, so that we can freely take
p^{\mathbb{N}}
-th power of the generators
A_{i}
.

For any
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module
\mathcal{N}
, let
\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{N})
denote the set of all
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear maps from
\mathcal{N}
to
\mathcal{N}
, then
\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{N})
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra.
Furthermore, let
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{N})
denote the set of all
invertible

\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear maps from
\mathcal{N}
to
\mathcal{N}
.
In particular, if
\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
, then
\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{N})
and
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{N})
are respectively the matrix sets
\mathsf{M}_{d\times d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
and
\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.
In general, for
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{e}}
, the structures of
\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
and
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
are more complicated.

Since
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
are invertible, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
is an
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module, each
A_{i},i=1,\ldots,N
, can be considered as an element of
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
by the map

\displaystyle A_{i}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto A_{i}\cdot v.

Furthermore,
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
commute pairwise.

As in Lemma
3.17
, for
i=1,\ldots,e
, let
\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{id_{i}}\}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-basis of the component
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
in the decomposition (
3.22
).
Then, the set
\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{11},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1d_{1}},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{e1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{ed_{e}}\}
generates
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module.
For any
f\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
and each
k=1,\ldots,e;l=1,\ldots,d_{k}
, the element
f\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{kl}
can be written uniquely as a sum

f\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{kl}=\sum_{i=1}^{e}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{i}}z_{ij,kl}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{ij},

(3.23)

where

z_{ij,kl}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\quad\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,e,\;j=1,\ldots,d_{i}.

(3.24)

Furthermore, since

p^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{e}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{i}}z_{ij,kl}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{ij}=p^{k}(f\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{kl})=f\cdot(p^{k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{kl})=0,

we must have

p^{i-k}\mid z_{ij,kl}\quad\text{ for all }i>k,\;j=1,\ldots,d_{i}.

(3.25)

It is easy to see that any tuple
\left(z_{ij,kl}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,e;j=1,\ldots,d_{i};k=1,\ldots,e;l=1,\ldots,d_{k}}
satisfying (
3.24
) and (
3.25
) defines an element
f\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, by extending
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linearly the Definition (
3.23
) from the basis
\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{11},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1d_{1}},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{e1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{ed_{e}}\}
to the whole module
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
.

Note that
\left(z_{ij,kl}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,e;j=1,\ldots,d_{i};k=1,\ldots,e;l=1,\ldots,d_{k}}
, are the coefficients of
f
in its matrix form under the basis
\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{11},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1d_{1}},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{e1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{ed_{e}}\}
.
From now on, for any
f\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
and
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,e\}
, we will let

f_{ik}\coloneqq\left(z_{ij,kl}\right)_{j=1,\ldots,d_{i};l=1,\ldots,d_{k}}\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{i}\times d_{k}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

denote the
(i,k)
-th block of
f
.
Taking into account the divisibility constraints (
3.25
), the map
f
can be written as a block matrix

\begin{pmatrix}M_{11}&M_{12}&M_{13}&\cdots&M_{1e}\\
pM_{21}&M_{22}&M_{23}&\cdots&M_{2e}\\
p^{2}M_{31}&pM_{32}&M_{33}&\cdots&M_{3e}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
p^{e-1}M_{e1}&p^{e-2}M_{e2}&p^{e-3}M_{e3}&\cdots&M_{ee}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(3.26)

where
M_{ik}\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{i}\times d_{k}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
for all
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,e\}
.
Then,
f_{ik}=p^{i-k}M_{ik}
for
i>k
, and
f_{ik}=M_{ik}
for
i\leq k
.
Note that for any
f\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, the blocks
f_{ik}
are uniquely defined, but the choices of matrices
M_{ik},i>k
are not unique.

For two endomorphisms
f,g\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, their composition
gf
can be computed by multiplying their corresponding matrices.
That is,

(gf)_{ik}=\sum_{j=1}^{e}\left(g_{ij}f_{jk}\mod p^{i}\right).

Note that although
f_{jk}
has coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{j}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, the expression
\left(g_{ij}f_{jk}\mod p^{i}\right)
is well defined even when
i>j
.
Indeed, when
i>j
, we have
p^{i-j}\mid g_{ij}
, so

f^{\prime}_{jk}\equiv f^{\prime\prime}_{jk}\mod p^{j}\implies g_{ij}f^{\prime}_{jk}\equiv g_{ij}f^{\prime\prime}_{jk}\mod p^{i}.

The next lemma shows that if
f
is invertible, then so are its diagonal blocks.

Lemma 3.18
.

If
f\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
then
M_{11}\in\mathsf{GL}_{d_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)),\;M_{22}\in\mathsf{GL}_{d_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{2}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)),\;\ldots,\;M_{ee}\in\mathsf{GL}_{d_{e}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.

Proof.

Suppose
f\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
.
Write
f^{-1}
as the matrix.

\begin{pmatrix}M^{\prime}_{11}&M^{\prime}_{12}&M^{\prime}_{13}&\cdots&M^{\prime}_{1e}\\
pM^{\prime}_{21}&M^{\prime}_{22}&M^{\prime}_{23}&\cdots&M^{\prime}_{2e}\\
p^{2}M^{\prime}_{31}&pM^{\prime}_{32}&M^{\prime}_{33}&\cdots&M^{\prime}_{3e}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
p^{e-1}M^{\prime}_{e1}&p^{e-2}M^{\prime}_{e2}&p^{e-3}M^{\prime}_{e3}&\cdots&M^{\prime}_{ee}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Then computing the product
f\cdot f^{-1}
modulo
p
, we obtain
M_{11}M^{\prime}_{11}\equiv I\mod p,\ldots,M_{ee}M^{\prime}_{ee}\equiv I\mod p
.
This shows that
M_{11},\ldots,M_{ee}
are invertible modulo
p
.
We claim that for any
a\geq 1
, invertibility of a matrix
M\in\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
modulo
p^{a}
implies invertibility modulo
p^{a+1}
.
Indeed, suppose
MM^{\prime}\equiv I\mod p^{a}
, then writing
MM^{\prime}=I+p^{a}A
, we have

M(M^{\prime}-p^{a}M^{\prime}A)=MM^{\prime}-p^{a}MM^{\prime}A=(I+p^{a}A)-p^{a}(I+p^{a}A)A\equiv I\mod p^{2a}.

Therefore
M
is invertible modulo
p^{a+1}\mid p^{2a}
.
Applying this iteratively for
a=1,2,\ldots
, we conclude that
M_{11}\in\mathsf{GL}_{d_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)),\ldots,M_{ee}\in\mathsf{GL}_{d_{e}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.
∎

The main idea of proving Proposition
3.16
is to simultaneously block-diagonalize the matrix forms of
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
, up to taking
p
-th powers.
There are two main difficulties.
The first is that the matrix blocks do not commute, nor do they have the same dimension.
The second is that the diagonalization can only use conjugators in
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, which have certain divisibility constraints for its blocks in the lower-left part of the matrix.
For these reasons, we will perform the diagonalization step by step on its blocks.
First, we simultaneously lower-triangularize the matrices
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
.
Some of the arguments used in the following step will also appear in the next subsection.

Step 2: simultaneous block lower-triangularization.

In this step, we simultaneously lower-triangularize the matrices
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
, up to taking their
p^{\mathbb{N}}
-th powers.
More precisely, we will show the following.

Proposition 3.19

(simultaneous block lower-triangularization)
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
be pairwise commuting elements of
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
.
Then there exist effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, such that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are block lower-triangular (i.e. their
(j,k)
-th blocks are zero for all
1\leq j<k\leq e
).

To prove Proposition
3.19
, we will increase the
p
-adic valuation of the upper-right blocks step by step.
Let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq b<c\leq e
.
We say that an element
f\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is
(a,b,c)
-triangular
, if it satisfies

\displaystyle p^{a}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }1\leq j<k\leq e,

\displaystyle p^{a+1}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }1\leq j<k\leq e,\;k\geq c+1,

\displaystyle p^{a+1}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }b<j<c,\;k=c.

(3.27)

This is in addition to the divisibility constraints (
3.25
) that all elements of
\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
are subject to.
In this case,
f
can be written in the matrix form

\begin{pmatrix}M_{11}&p^{a}M_{12}&\cdots&&&p^{a}M_{1c}&p^{a+1}M_{1(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&\ddots&&&&\vdots&\vdots\\
&&M_{bb}&&&p^{a}M_{bc}&p^{a+1}M_{b(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&\ddots&&p^{a+1}M_{(b+1)c}&p^{a+1}M_{(b+1)(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
&*&&&&M_{cc}&p^{a+1}M_{c(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&&&\ddots&\cdots\\
\end{pmatrix},

where
*
denotes entries satisfying the divisibility constraints (
3.25
).
In particular, every entry represented by
*
is divisible by
p
.
Note that if
b+1=c
then the term
p^{a+1}M_{(b+1)c}
is overwritten by
M_{cc}
.

Observation 3.20
.

The composition of two
(a,b,c)
-triangular automorphisms is
(a,b,c)
-triangular.

Lemma 3.21

(single step of triangularization)
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
be pairwise commuting elements of
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq b<c\leq e
.
Suppose that
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
are
(a,b,c)
-triangular.
Then there exist effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, such that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are
(a,b,c)
-triangular and their
(b,c)
-th blocks are divisible by
p^{a+1}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality suppose
a<b
. Otherwise
a\geq b
, so for all
f\in\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}\}
, we have
f_{bc}\in p^{a}\cdot\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{b}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))\subseteq p^{b}\cdot\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{b}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))=\{0\}
.
This means that the
(b,c)
-th blocks of
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
are zero, and hence divisible by
p^{a+1}
.
So we can take
R
to be the identity and
\ell=0
, and there is nothing to prove.

Let
A_{i}\in\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}\}
.
Since
A_{i}
is
(a,b,c)
-triangular, it can be written in the matrix form

\begin{pmatrix}M_{i11}&p^{a}M_{i12}&\cdots&&&p^{a}M_{i1c}&p^{a+1}M_{i1(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&\ddots&&&&\vdots&\vdots\\
&&M_{ibb}&&&p^{a}M_{ibc}&p^{a+1}M_{ib(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&\ddots&&p^{a+1}M_{i(b+1)c}&p^{a+1}M_{i(b+1)(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
&*&&&&M_{icc}&p^{a+1}M_{ic(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&&&\ddots&\cdots\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We claim that
A_{i}^{m}
can be written in the form

\begin{pmatrix}M^{\prime}_{i11}&p^{a}M^{\prime}_{i12}&\cdots&&&p^{a}M^{\prime}_{i1c}&p^{a+1}M^{\prime}_{i1(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&\ddots&&&&\vdots&\vdots\\
&&M^{\prime}_{ibb}&&&p^{a}M^{\prime}_{ibc}&p^{a+1}M^{\prime}_{ib(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&\ddots&&p^{a+1}M^{\prime}_{i(b+1)c}&p^{a+1}M^{\prime}_{i(b+1)(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
&*&&&&M^{\prime}_{icc}&p^{a+1}M^{\prime}_{ic(c+1)}&\cdots\\
&&&&&&\ddots&\cdots\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(3.28)

where

\left(A_{i}^{m}\right)_{jj}=M^{\prime}_{ijj}\equiv M_{ijj}^{m}\mod p,\quad j=1,\ldots,e,

(3.29)

and
\left(A_{i}^{m}\right)_{bc}=p^{a}M^{\prime}_{ibc}
with

M^{\prime}_{ibc}\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}M_{ibb}^{k}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{m-1-k}\mod p.

(3.30)

Indeed, the congruence (
3.29
) is obvious from the fact that
A_{i}
is block upper-triangular modulo
p
.
(Recall that every entry represented by
*
is divisible by
p
).
The congruence (
3.30
) follows by induction on
m
the following way.
For
m=1
, the congruence (
3.30
) is obvious.
Suppose (
3.30
) holds for
m
, then
A_{i}^{m+1}\equiv A_{i}\cdot A_{i}^{m}\mod p^{a+1}
yields the recurrence

\displaystyle\left(A_{i}^{m+1}\right)_{bc}

\displaystyle\equiv p\cdot p^{a}(\cdots)+M_{ibb}\left(A_{i}^{m}\right)_{bc}+p^{a}\cdot p^{a+1}(\cdots)+p^{a}M_{ibc}\left(A_{i}^{m}\right)_{cc}+p^{a+1}\cdot p\cdot(\cdots)

\displaystyle\equiv M_{ibb}\left(A_{i}^{m}\right)_{bc}+p^{a}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{m}\mod p^{a+1}.

Therefore for
m+1
, we have

p^{a}M^{\prime}_{ibc}\equiv M_{ibb}\left(p^{a}\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}M_{ibb}^{k}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{m-1-k}\right)+p^{a}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{m}\equiv p^{a}\sum_{k=0}^{m}M_{ibb}^{k}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{m-k}\mod p^{a+1},

which yields the congruence (
3.30
) for
m+1
.

We now take
m\coloneqq p^{\ell}
and consider the matrices
A_{i}^{p^{\ell}},i=1,\ldots,N
.
Let
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
be the automorphism defined by

R_{jk}=\begin{cases}I\quad&1\leq j=k\leq e,\\
p^{a}Q\quad&j=b,k=c,\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

for some
Q\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{b}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
to be determined later.
That is,

R=\begin{pmatrix}I&0&\cdots&&&0&0&\cdots\\
&\ddots&&&&\vdots&\vdots\\
&&I&&&p^{a}Q&0&\cdots\\
&&&\ddots&&0&0&\cdots\\
&&&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&0&&&&I&0&\cdots\\
&&&&&&\ddots&\cdots\\
\end{pmatrix},\quad R^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}I&0&\cdots&&&0&0&\cdots\\
&\ddots&&&&\vdots&\vdots\\
&&I&&&-p^{a}Q&0&\cdots\\
&&&\ddots&&0&0&\cdots\\
&&&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&0&&&&I&0&\cdots\\
&&&&&&\ddots&\cdots\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Both
R,R^{-1}
are
(a,b,c)
-triangular, and consequently all
R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R,i=1,\ldots,N,
are
(a,b,c)
-triangular.
We want to find
Q
such that
p^{a+1}\mid\left(R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\right)_{bc}
for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.

From the matrix form (
3.28
), we can directly compute

\left(R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\right)_{bc}\equiv p^{a}\left(M^{\prime}_{ibc}-QM^{\prime}_{icc}+M^{\prime}_{ibb}Q\right)\equiv p^{a}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}M_{ibb}^{k}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{p^{\ell}-1-k}-QM_{icc}^{p^{\ell}}+M_{ibb}^{p^{\ell}}Q\right)\\
\mod p^{a+1}.

Therefore in order for
p^{a+1}\mid\left(R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\right)_{bc}
to be satisfied, it suffices to find

(Q\mod p)\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

such that

\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}M_{ibb}^{k}M_{ibc}M_{icc}^{p^{\ell}-1-k}\equiv QM_{icc}^{p^{\ell}}-M_{ibb}^{p^{\ell}}Q\mod p.

(3.31)

We now without loss of generality write
Q
instead of
(Q\mod p)
.

For each
i=1,\ldots,N
, let
\varphi_{i}
denote the
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformation

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}\colon\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto SM_{icc}-M_{ibb}S.

Here,
\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
is considered as a
d_{b}d_{c}
-dimensional vector space over
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
.
By Lemma
3.22
below, Equation (
3.31
) can be rewritten as

\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{ibc})=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(Q).

(3.32)

For all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, since
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
commute, we have

\displaystyle M_{ibb}M_{jbb}\equiv(A_{i}A_{j})_{bb}

\displaystyle\equiv(A_{j}A_{i})_{bb}\equiv M_{jbb}M_{ibb}\mod p,

\displaystyle M_{icc}M_{jcc}\equiv(A_{i}A_{j})_{cc}

\displaystyle\equiv(A_{j}A_{i})_{cc}\equiv M_{jcc}M_{icc}\mod p,

\displaystyle p^{a}(M_{ibb}M_{jbc}+M_{ibc}M_{jcc})\equiv(A_{i}A_{j})_{bc}

\displaystyle\equiv(A_{j}A_{i})_{bc}\equiv p^{a}(M_{jbb}M_{ibc}+M_{jbc}M_{icc})\mod p^{a+1}.

Therefore for all
S\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
, we have

\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}(S)=SM_{jcc}M_{icc}-M_{jbb}SM_{icc}-M_{ibb}SM_{jcc}+M_{ibb}M_{jbb}S\\
=SM_{icc}M_{jcc}-M_{jbb}SM_{icc}-M_{ibb}SM_{jcc}+M_{jbb}M_{ibb}S=\varphi_{j}\varphi_{i}(S).

Hence,
\varphi_{i}
and
\varphi_{j}
commute for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Furthermore,

\varphi_{i}(M_{jbc})=M_{jbc}M_{icc}-M_{ibb}M_{jbc}\equiv M_{ibc}M_{jcc}-M_{jbb}M_{ibc}=\varphi_{j}(M_{ibc})\mod p.

Hence
\varphi_{i}(M_{jbc})=\varphi_{j}(M_{ibc})
for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
By Lemma
3.23
below, we can compute
Q\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{b}\times d_{c}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that

\varphi_{1}^{p^{\ell}}(Q)=\varphi_{1}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{1bc}),\ldots,\varphi_{N}^{p^{\ell}}(Q)=\varphi_{N}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{Nbc}).

We have thus found
Q
that satisfies Equation (
3.32
) (and hence Equation (
3.31
)) for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
d,d^{\prime}
be positive integers.
Let
C\in\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)),B\in\mathsf{GL}_{d^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.
Define the
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformation

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\mathsf{M}_{d\times d^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathsf{M}_{d\times d^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto MC-BM.

Then for any
\ell\geq 1
, we have

\varphi^{p^{\ell}-1}(M)=\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}B^{k}MC^{p^{\ell}-1-k},

(3.33)

and

\varphi^{p^{\ell}}(M)=MC^{p^{\ell}}-B^{p^{\ell}}M.

(3.34)

Proof.

We prove by induction on
m
that

\varphi^{m}(M)=\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k}MC^{m-k}.

(3.35)

For
m=1
, Equation (
3.35
) holds by the definition of
\varphi
.
Suppose Equation (
3.35
) holds for
m
, then

\displaystyle\varphi^{m+1}(M)

\displaystyle=\varphi\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k}MC^{m-k}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k}MC^{m-k}\right)C-B\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k}MC^{m-k}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k}MC^{m+1-k}-\sum_{k=0}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}B^{k+1}MC^{m-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m+1}\left((-1)^{k}\binom{m}{k}-(-1)^{k-1}\binom{m}{k-1}\right)B^{k}MC^{m+1-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m+1}(-1)^{k}\left(\binom{m}{k}+\binom{m}{k-1}\right)B^{k}MC^{m+1-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m+1}(-1)^{k}\binom{m+1}{k}B^{k}MC^{m+1-k}.

This proves Equation (
3.35
) for
m+1
.

Specializing Equation (
3.35
) for
m=p^{\ell}-1
and noticing

(-1)^{k}\binom{p^{\ell}-1}{k}\equiv(-1)^{k}\cdot\frac{p^{\ell}-1}{1}\cdot\frac{p^{\ell}-2}{2}\cdots\frac{p^{\ell}-k}{k}\equiv 1\mod p

for prime
p
, we obtain (
3.33
).

Specializing Equation (
3.35
) for
m=p^{\ell}
.
By Lucas’ theorem
[
Gra97
]
, we have

\binom{p^{\ell}}{k}\equiv\begin{cases}1\mod p,\quad k=0\text{ or }p^{\ell},\\
0\mod p,\quad 1\leq k\leq p^{\ell}-1.\end{cases}

Thus we obtain (
3.34
).
∎

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
D
be a positive integer and
V
be a
D
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-vector space.
Let
M_{1},\ldots,M_{N}\in V
, and let
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{N}
be pairwise commuting elements of
\operatorname{End}(V)=\mathsf{M}_{d\times d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
, such that

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}(M_{j})=\varphi_{j}(M_{i})

for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Then there exist effectively computable
Q\in V
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\varphi_{1}^{p^{\ell}}(Q)=\varphi_{1}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{N}^{p^{\ell}}(Q)=\varphi_{N}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{N}).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be the
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-subalgebra of
\operatorname{End}(V)
generated by
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{N}
and the identity element.
Since the endomorphisms
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{N}
commute pairwise,
\mathcal{F}
is commutative.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{F}
has finite dimension over
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, since
\operatorname{End}(V)
has finite dimension over
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{F}
is Artinian.

By
[
Eis13
, Corollary 2.16]
, the Artinian ring
\mathcal{F}
can be decomposed into a direct product of local rings

\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{R}_{1}\times\mathcal{R}_{2}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{R}_{q}.

Let

\mathcal{F}_{i}\coloneqq\{0\}\times\cdots\times\{0\}\times\mathcal{R}_{i}\times\{0\}\times\cdots\times\{0\}

for
i=1,\ldots,q
.
Since
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{i}
, each
\mathcal{F}_{i}
is a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{F}
.
This means that
\mathcal{F}
is a direct sum of the local
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-subalgebras
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{q}
:

\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{2}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{F}_{q}.

(3.36)

See
[
BBC+96
, Section 3.2, p.501]
for an effective algorithm for finding the decomposition (
3.36
).
For each
i
, the maximal ideal of the local ring
\mathcal{F}_{i}
is

\operatorname{rad}(\mathcal{F}_{i})\coloneqq\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{i}\mid\exists m\geq 1,f^{m}=0\}.

Indeed,
\operatorname{rad}(\mathcal{F}_{i})
is the intersection of all prime ideals of
\mathcal{F}_{i}

[
AM69
, Proposition 1.8]
, which is exactly the maximal ideal of the Artinian local ring
\mathcal{F}_{i}

[
AM69
, Proposition 8.1]
.
Furthermore, there exists an effectively computable
t_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
\operatorname{rad}(\mathcal{F}_{i})^{t_{i}}=0

[
AM69
, Proposition 8.6]
.

For any
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, since
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
, we can write

\varphi_{i}=(f_{i1},f_{i2},\ldots,f_{iq})\in\mathcal{F}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{2}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{F}_{q}

according to this decomposition.
Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
p^{\ell}\geq 1+\max\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{q}\}
.

Since
\mathcal{F}
acts
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linearly on
V
, we can decompose

V=V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{q}

according to the decomposition of
\mathcal{F}
, that is,
V_{1}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}_{1}\cdot V
,
\ldots
,
V_{q}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}_{q}\cdot V
.
We write each
M_{i}\in V,i=1,\ldots,N,
as

M_{i}=(v_{i1},\ldots,v_{iq})\in V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{q}

according to this decomposition.
Then for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, the equality
\varphi_{i}(M_{j})=\varphi_{j}(M_{i})
implies

f_{i1}v_{j1}=f_{j1}v_{i1},\ldots,f_{iq}v_{jq}=f_{jq}v_{iq}
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{1}\coloneqq\{i\mid 1\leq i\leq N,f_{i1}\notin\operatorname{rad}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\}
.
Since
f_{i1}v_{j1}=f_{j1}v_{i1}
for all
i,j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
, we have
f_{i1}^{-1}v_{i1}=f_{j1}^{-1}v_{j1}
for all
i,j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
.
Therefore, there exists
v_{1}^{*}\in V_{1}
such that
v_{1}^{*}=f_{i1}^{-1}v_{i1}
for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
.
This yields
f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}}v_{1}^{*}=f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{i1}
for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
.
For all
i\notin\mathcal{I}_{1}
, we have
f_{i1}^{t_{1}}=0
, so
f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}}v_{1}^{*}=0=f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{i1}
since
p^{\ell}\geq 1+\max\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{q}\}
.
In both cases, we have

f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}}v_{1}^{*}=f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{i1}.

Similarly, we can find
v_{2}^{*}\in V_{1},\ldots,v_{q}^{*}\in V_{q}
, such that

f_{i2}^{p^{\ell}}v_{2}^{*}=f_{i2}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{i2}\;,\;\ldots\;,\;f_{iq}^{p^{\ell}}v_{q}^{*}=f_{iq}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{iq},

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Let

Q\coloneqq(v_{1}^{*},\ldots,v_{q}^{*})\in V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{q},

then for all
i=1,\ldots,N
, we have

\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(Q)=(f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}}v_{1}^{*},\ldots,f_{iq}^{p^{\ell}}v_{q}^{*})=(f_{i1}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{i1},\ldots,f_{iq}^{p^{\ell}-1}v_{iq})=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{i}).

∎

To prove Proposition
3.19
, we will apply Lemma
3.21
repeatedly.
Observe that if
A\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is
(a,b,c)
-triangular and its
(b,c)
-th block is divisible by
p^{a+1}
, then
A
is

\begin{cases}\text{$(a,b-1,c)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b>1$,}\\
\text{$(a,c-2,c-1)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b=1,c>2$,}\\
\text{$(a+1,e-1,e)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b=1,c=2$.}\\
\end{cases}

See
3.19

Proof.

Apply Lemma
3.21
repeatedly for

\displaystyle(a,b,c)=\;

\displaystyle(0,e-1,e),(0,e-2,e),\ldots,(0,1,e),

\displaystyle(0,e-2,e-1),(0,e-3,e-1),\ldots,(0,1,e-1),

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle(0,2,3),(0,1,3),

\displaystyle(0,1,2),

the following way.
We start with the matrices
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
, which are
(0,e-1,e)
-triangular (every element in
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is
(0,e-1,e)
-triangular).
After each application of Lemma
3.21
, we obtain the matrices
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
, and we apply the next repetition of Lemma
3.21
on
A_{1}\coloneqq R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,A_{N}\coloneqq R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
.
Since

{R^{\prime}}^{-1}\left(R^{-1}A^{p^{\ell}}R\right)^{p^{\ell^{\prime}}}R^{\prime}={R^{\prime}}^{-1}R^{-1}A^{p^{\ell}+p^{\ell^{\prime}}}RR^{\prime}=(RR^{\prime})^{-1}A^{p^{\ell+\ell^{\prime}}}(RR^{\prime}),

the repeated application of Lemma
3.21
yields

\widehat{\ell}\coloneqq\ell+\ell^{\prime}+\cdots\in\mathbb{N},\quad\widehat{R}\coloneqq RR^{\prime}\cdots\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}),

such that the
(j,k)
-th blocks of
\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R},\ldots,\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R}
are divisible by
p
for all
1\leq j<k\leq e
.

Then starting from the matrices
A_{1}\coloneqq\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R},\ldots,A_{N}\coloneqq\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R}
, and apply Lemma
3.40
repeated for

\displaystyle(a,b,c)=\;

\displaystyle(1,e-1,e),(1,e-2,e),\ldots,(1,1,e),

\displaystyle(1,e-2,e-1),(1,e-3,e-1),\ldots,(1,1,e-1),

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle(1,2,3),(1,1,3),

\displaystyle(1,1,2),

we can find
\widehat{\ell},\widehat{R}
such that the
(j,k)
-th blocks of
\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R},\ldots,\widehat{R}^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\widehat{\ell}}}\widehat{R}
are divisible by
p^{2}
for all
1\leq j<k\leq e
.

Repeat the above process for
a=2,3,\ldots,e-1
. Then we find
\ell,R
, such that the
(j,k)
-th blocks of
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R
,
\ldots
,
R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are divisible by
p^{e}=0
for all
1\leq j<k\leq e
.
Thus
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are block lower-triangular.
∎

Step 3: simultaneous block diagonalization.

In this step, we simultaneously diagonalize the matrices
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
, up to taking their
p^{\mathbb{N}}
-th powers.
More precisely, we will show the following.

Proposition 3.24

(simultaneous block diagonalization)
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
be pairwise commuting elements of
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
.
Then there exist effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, such that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are block-diagonal (i.e. their
(j,k)
-th blocks are zero for all
j\neq k
).

Following the previous step, we can suppose
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
to be already block lower-triangularized.
That is, we can replace
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
with the elements
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
obtained from Proposition
3.19
.

The proof of Proposition
3.24
is similar to that of Proposition
3.19
.
Namely, we will increase the
p
-adic valuation of the lower-right blocks step by step.
Let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq b<c\leq e
.
We say that an element
f\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is
(a,b,c)
-diagonal
, if it satisfies

\displaystyle f_{jk}=0

\displaystyle\text{ for all }1\leq j<k\leq e,

\displaystyle p^{a}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }1\leq k<j\leq e,

\displaystyle p^{a+1}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }1\leq k<j\leq e,\;j\geq c+1,

\displaystyle p^{a+1}\mid f_{jk}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }b<k<c,\;j=c.

(3.37)

in addition to the divisibility constraints (
3.25
) which all elements of
\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
are subject to.
In this case,
f
can be written in the matrix form

\begin{pmatrix}M_{11}&&&&&\\
p^{a}M_{21}&\ddots&&&&0\\
\vdots&&M_{bb}\\
&&&\ddots&\\
&&&&\ddots&\\
p^{a}M_{c1}&\cdots&p^{a}M_{cb}&p^{a+1}M_{c(b+1)}&\cdots&M_{cc}\\
p^{a+1}M_{(c+1)1}&\cdots&p^{a+1}M_{(c+1)b}&p^{a+1}M_{(c+1)(b+1)}&\cdots&p^{a+1}M_{(c+1)c}&\ddots\\
\vdots&&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots\\
\end{pmatrix}.

If
b+1=c
then the term
p^{a+1}M_{c(b+1)}
is overwritten by
M_{cc}
.

Observation 3.25
.

The composition of two
(a,b,c)
-diagonal automorphisms is
(a,b,c)
-diagonal.

Lemma 3.26

(single step of diagonalization)
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
be pairwise commuting elements of
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq b<c\leq e
.
Suppose that
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
are
(a,b,c)
-diagonal.
Then there exist effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, such that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are
(a,b,c)
-diagonal and their
(c,b)
-th blocks are divisible by
p^{a+1}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality suppose
c-b\leq a
.
Otherwise we have
c-b\geq a+1
, so
p^{a+1}\mid p^{c-b}
.
Since
p^{c-b}\mid f_{cb}
for all
f\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
by the divisibility constraint (
3.25
), we have
p^{a+1}\mid A_{i},i=1,\ldots,N
.
We can take
\ell=0
and
R
to be the identity map, and we have nothing to prove.

Let
R
be the block matrix defined by

R_{jk}=\begin{cases}I\quad&1\leq j=k\leq e,\\
-p^{a}Q\quad&j=c,k=b,\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

for some
Q\in\mathsf{M}_{d_{c}\times d_{b}}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{c}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
to be determined later.
That is,

R=\begin{pmatrix}I&&&&\\
0&\ddots&&&&0\\
\vdots&&I\\
&&&\ddots&\\
&&&&\ddots&\\
0&\cdots&-p^{a}Q&0&\cdots&I\\
0&\cdots&0&0&\cdots&0&\ddots\\
\vdots&&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots\\
\end{pmatrix}.

We now verify that
R
satisfies the divisibility constraints (
3.25
), so that
R
is indeed in
\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
.
Indeed, since
p^{a}\mid R_{cb},c-b\leq a
, we have
p^{c-b}\mid R_{cb}
.
Therefore
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
.

The rest of the proof is the same as Lemma
3.21
, but with all the matrices transposed.
∎

To prove Proposition
3.24
, we will apply Lemma
3.26
repeatedly.
Observe that if
A\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is
(a,b,c)
-diagonal and its
(c,b)
-th block is divisible by
p^{a+1}
, then
A
is

\begin{cases}\text{$(a,b-1,c)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b>1$,}\\
\text{$(a,c-2,c-1)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b=1,c>2$,}\\
\text{$(a+1,e-1,e)$-triangular,}\quad&\text{if $b=1,c=2$.}\\
\end{cases}

See
3.24

Proof.

By Proposition
3.19
, we can without loss of generality suppose
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
to be block lower-triangular.
The rest of the proof is the same as Proposition
3.19
.
Apply Lemma
3.26
repeatedly for

\displaystyle(a,b,c)=\;

\displaystyle(a,e-1,e),(a,e-2,e),\ldots,(a,1,e),

\displaystyle(a,e-2,e-1),(a,e-3,e-1),\ldots,(a,1,e-1),

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle(a,2,3),(a,1,3),

\displaystyle(a,1,2),

for
a=0,1,\ldots,e-1
. We can find
\ell,R
such that the
(j,k)
-th blocks of
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R
,
\ldots
,
R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are divisible by
p^{e}=0
for all
1\leq k<j\leq e
and all
1\leq j<k\leq e
.
This means that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are block diagonal.
∎

Step 4: reduction to S-unit equations over the
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
.

Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{1}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{2}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{2}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{e}}

(3.38)

be the decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module specified in Lemma
3.17
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}
denote respectively the components
\mathbb{Z}_{/p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{1}},\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{2}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{2}},\ldots,\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{e}}
in (
3.38
).

By Proposition
3.24
, we can compute
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
, such that
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are block diagonal.
This means that

R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\;R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2},\;\ldots,\;R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e},

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Since
R^{-1}A_{1}^{p^{\ell}}R,\ldots,R^{-1}A_{N}^{p^{\ell}}R
are invertible, we have

R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}=\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\;R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}=\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2},\;\ldots,\;R^{-1}A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}=\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}.

Therefore

A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\;A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2},\;\ldots,\;A_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Since
A_{i}^{p^{\ell}},A_{i}^{-p^{\ell}},i=1,\ldots,N
, generate
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra (see property (iv) of Proposition
3.7
), we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\;\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2},\;\ldots,\;\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\cdot R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}.

This means that we have the decomposition

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\oplus R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}\oplus\cdots\oplus R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{e}

(3.39)

as an
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module.

Let
\pi_{i}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i},i=1,\ldots,e
, be the projections according to the decomposition (
3.39
).
Then the solution set of the an equation

A_{1}^{z_{11}}A_{2}^{z_{12}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{1N}}\cdot v_{1}+\cdots+A_{1}^{z_{K1}}A_{2}^{z_{K2}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{KN}}\cdot v_{K}=v_{0}

(3.40)

over the
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
is equal to the intersection of the solution set of equations

A_{1}^{z_{11}}A_{2}^{z_{12}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{1N}}\cdot\pi_{i}(v_{1})+\cdots+A_{1}^{z_{K1}}A_{2}^{z_{K2}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{KN}}\cdot\pi_{i}(v_{K})=\pi_{i}(v_{0})

(3.41)

over the
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-modules
R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\;i=1,\ldots,e
.

Since
R\in\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})
is injective, the map
R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i},v\mapsto R^{-1}v
defines an isomorphism between
R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Therefore

R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}

for
i=1,\ldots,e
.
We consider the S-unit Equations (
3.41
) over the
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq R\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
.
Since
p^{i}\cdot\mathcal{V}=0
, the
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module
\mathcal{V}
is actually an
\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
-module.
Hence, Equation (
3.41
) can be considered as an S-unit equation over the
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{V}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d_{i}}
, by replacing
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
with their image under the projection
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{A}
.

This completes all the ingredients for the proof of Proposition
3.16
:

See
3.16

Proof.

It suffices to show that the ring
\mathcal{A}
and the
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{V}
satisfy the properties (i)-(iv).
Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
is local with some maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}
, the quotient
\mathcal{A}=\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
is also local with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{p}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
.
Furthermore, since
\mathfrak{p}^{t}=0
for some
t
, we have
\mathfrak{m}^{t}=0
for the same
t
.
This proves the property (i) of
\mathcal{A}
in Proposition
3.16
.
Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
is a effectively represented as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra, the quotient
\mathcal{A}=\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}/p^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
is a effectively represented as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)/p^{i}\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra
This shows property (ii).
Property (iii) of
\mathcal{A}
is inherited from the property (iii) of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
from Proposition
3.7
.
Taking
d=d_{i}
, we can write
\mathcal{V}
as
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{i}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
, this yields property (iv).
∎

From now on, we focus on S-unit equations

A_{1}^{z_{11}}A_{2}^{z_{12}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{1N}}\cdot v_{1}+\cdots+A_{1}^{z_{K1}}A_{2}^{z_{K2}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{KN}}\cdot v_{K}=v_{0}

in
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{V}
.
To re-uniformize our notation, we replace the exponent
i
with
e
, so that
\mathcal{A}
is again a local
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra, and
\mathcal{V}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module for some
d\geq 1
.
In particular, each invertible element
A\in\mathcal{A}
acts on
\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
as a matrix in
\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.
From now on, we denote by
\mathfrak{m}
the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{A}
.

3.4 Pseudo Frobenius splitting

As illustrated in Example
3.2
, the key part in Derksen and Masser’s proof of Theorem
1.1
is the so-called
Frobenius splitting
.
Recall that this means for a field
\mathbb{K}
of characteristic
p
,

\mathbb{K}^{p}\coloneqq\{k^{p}\mid k\in\mathbb{K}\}

is a subfield of
\mathbb{K}
, making
\mathbb{K}
an
\mathbb{K}^{p}
-vector space.
For the special case of the field
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, we have
f(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})^{p}=f(X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X_{n}^{p})
.
Therefore

\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p}=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})\coloneqq\{f(X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X_{n}^{p})\mid f\in\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\}

is a subfield of
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, and
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
splits as an direct sum of
p^{n}
different
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})
-vector spaces:

\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\bigoplus_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}.

(3.42)

Ideally, we would like to have a similar result for the algebra
\mathcal{A}
.
However, the situation here is more delicate, namely
\mathcal{A}^{p}\coloneqq\{a^{p}\mid a\in\mathcal{A}\}
is not necessarily a subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}
(we no longer have
a^{p}+b^{p}=(a+b)^{p}
).
Therefore, we need to generalize the Frobenius splitting from a field
\mathbb{K}
to the algebra
\mathcal{A}
, the same way Example
3.3
generalizes Example
3.2
.
In this subsection, we will construct such a generalization, which we will call
pseudo Frobenius splitting
(Proposition
3.28
).

First, we show that there is a splitting for
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
similar to the splitting for
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
in Equation (
3.42
):

Lemma 3.27
.

Define
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})\coloneqq\{f(X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X_{n}^{p})\mid f\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\}
.
We have

\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\bigoplus_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}

as a
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})
-module.

Proof.

Consider the
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})
-linear map

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\quad\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})^{p^{n}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),

\displaystyle(f_{0,0,\ldots,0},f_{0,0,\ldots,1},\ldots,f_{p-1,p-1,\ldots,p-1})

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}f_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}.

First we show that
\varphi
is injective.
Suppose
\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}f_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}=0
.
Write each
f_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}=\frac{g_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}}{h_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}}
where
g_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}},h_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p}]
and
p\nmid h_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}
.
Then

0=\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}f_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}=\frac{\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}G_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}}{\prod_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}h_{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n}}},

where
G_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}\coloneqq g_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}\prod_{(r^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,r^{\prime}_{n})\neq(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}h_{r^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,r^{\prime}_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p}]
.
Therefore

0=\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}G_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}},

so we must have
G_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}=0
for all
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}
.
Consequently
g_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}=0
, because
h_{r^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,r^{\prime}_{n}}\neq 0
. We conclude that
f_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}=0
for all
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}
.

Next we show that
\varphi
is surjective.
Let
\frac{g}{h}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
where
g,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
with
p\nmid h
.
By Lemma
3.13
, we have
h^{p^{e}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p}]
.
Therefore,
\frac{g}{h}=\frac{gh^{p^{e}-1}}{h^{p^{e}}}
where
p\nmid h^{p^{e}}
and
h^{p^{e}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p}]
.
We can write
gh^{p^{e}-1}
as
\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}H_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}
with each
H_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p}]
.
Then

\frac{g}{h}=\frac{gh^{p^{e}-1}}{h^{p^{e}}}=\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}\frac{H_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}}{h^{p^{e}}}\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}=\varphi\left(\frac{H_{0,\ldots,0}}{h^{p^{e}}},\ldots,\frac{H_{p-1,\ldots,p-1}}{h^{p^{e}}}\right).

Therefore
\varphi
is surjective.

We conclude that
\varphi
is a bijection, so
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\bigoplus_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p})\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}
.
∎

Instead of the usual
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module structure on
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, we can define a different
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module structure on
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
by the action

*\;\colon\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\times\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu),\quad f*m\coloneqq f(X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X_{k}^{p})\cdot m.

We denote by
\Phi(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
this new
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-module.
Intuitively, applying
\Phi
to
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
can be considered as performing the “variable change”
X^{\prime}_{1}\coloneqq X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X^{\prime}_{n}\coloneqq X_{n}^{p}
, as in Example
3.2
or
3.3
.
By Lemma
3.27
, we have
\Phi\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)=\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)^{p^{n}}
.
For any element
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, it can be considered as an element in
\Phi\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)
which we denote by
\Phi(f)
.
In particular,
\Phi(f)=(f_{0,0,\ldots,0},f_{0,0,\ldots,1},\ldots,f_{p-1,p-1,\ldots,p-1})
, where
f_{r_{1},r_{2}\ldots,r_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
are such that

f=\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}}f_{r_{1},r_{2}\ldots,r_{n}}(X_{1}^{p},\ldots,X_{n}^{p})\cdot X_{1}^{r_{1}}X_{2}^{r_{2}}\cdots X_{n}^{r_{n}}.

We can extend the domain of definition of
\Phi
to
\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
, so that
\Phi(\mathcal{V})=\left(\Phi\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)\right)^{d}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}d}
.
In particular, if
\boldsymbol{v}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})\in\mathcal{V}
, then
\Phi(\boldsymbol{v})\coloneqq\left(\Phi(f_{1}),\ldots,\Phi(f_{d})\right)\in\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}\cdot d}=\Phi(\mathcal{V})
.
Let
A\in\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
be any invertible
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformation of
\mathcal{V}
.
Then
A
induces an invertible
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformation
\Phi(A)\colon\Phi(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow\Phi(\mathcal{V})
defined by
\Phi(A)\cdot\Phi(\boldsymbol{v})\coloneqq\Phi(A\boldsymbol{v})
.
In particular,
\Phi(A)\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.

Note that the map
\Phi
commutes with taking modulo
p
. More precisely, we have
\Phi(f+p\cdot\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))=\Phi(f)+p\cdot\Phi(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
, therefore
\Phi
induces a map from
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)/p\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
to
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}}/p\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}}=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}}
.
Similarly, we have
\Phi(\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V})=\Phi(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d})=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{n}d}
, and
\Phi(\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)))=\mathsf{GL}_{p^{n}d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.

One can also iterate the operation
\Phi
, and we denote
\Phi^{k}(\cdot)\coloneqq\underbrace{\Phi(\Phi(\cdots\Phi}_{k\text{ times }}(\cdot)\cdots))
.
Thus, we have the chains

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\;

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)^{p^{n}}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)^{p^{n}\cdot p^{n}}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\cdots

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)^{p^{n}d}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\left(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\right)^{p^{n}\cdot p^{n}d}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\cdots

\displaystyle\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{V})=\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\mathsf{GL}_{p^{n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\mathsf{GL}_{p^{2n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi\;\;}\;\cdots

In particular, each
\Phi^{k},k\geq 1
, is a bijection, and can be considered as performing the variable change
X^{\prime}_{1}\coloneqq X_{1}^{p^{k}},\ldots,X^{\prime}_{n}\coloneqq X_{n}^{p^{k}}
.
These also commute with taking modulo
p
.

We are now ready to construct the “pseudo Frobenius splitting” for the elements
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}\in\mathcal{A}
, considered as invertible matrices in
\mathsf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
.
The exact formulation is the following proposition.

Proposition 3.28

(Pseudo Frobenius splitting)
.

There exist an effectively computable integer
s\geq 0
, and an effectively computable matrix
R\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
, such that for all
i=1,\ldots,N
, we have

R^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\cdot R=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big).

(3.43)

Here,
\operatorname{diag}(A,\ldots,A)
denotes the block-diagonal matrix with the blocks
A,\ldots,A
on the diagonal.
The rest of this subsection will be dedicated to the proof of Proposition
3.28
.
The proof applies similar techniques to the block-diagonalization procedure from Subsection
3.3
.
Notably, Lemma
3.22
and Lemma
3.23
will be crucial.

Let
\mathcal{B}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}/p\mathcal{A}
. Then
\mathcal{B}
is a finite dimensional commutative
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra, which acts on
\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{d}
.
Since
\mathcal{A}
is local with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
satisfying
\mathfrak{m}^{t}=0
, the algebra
\mathcal{B}
is also local with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}/p\mathcal{A}
, and
(\mathfrak{m}/p\mathcal{A})^{t}=0
.
Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{N}
be the image of
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
in
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}/p\mathcal{A}
.
Since the map
\Phi
commutes with taking modulo
p
, it can be applied on the quotients
\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}/p\mathcal{A}
.

First, we show a special case of Proposition
3.28
for
e=1
: this is a rather classic extension of the Frobenius splitting.

Lemma 3.29

(Frobenius splitting of
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebras)
.

There exists an effectively computable integer
s\geq 0
, and an effectively computable matrix
R\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
, such that for all
i=1,\ldots,N
, we have

R^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\cdot R=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)

(3.44)

Proof.

Let
s
be such that
p^{s}\geq t
.
Since
\mathcal{B}
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-algebra, the set

\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}\coloneqq\{b^{p^{s}}\mid b\in\mathcal{B}\}

is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
-algebra.
We claim that
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
is an integral domain, and therefore a field
[
Eis13
, Corollary 4.7]
.
Indeed, suppose
xy=0
in
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}=(\mathcal{A}/p\mathcal{A})^{p^{s}}
, then
x=(v+p\mathcal{A})^{p^{s}},\;y=(w+p\mathcal{A})^{p^{s}}
for some
v,w\in\mathcal{A}
.
Then
xy=0
yields
v^{p^{s}}w^{p^{s}}\in p\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}
.
Since
\mathfrak{m}
is a prime ideal of
\mathcal{A}
, we have
v\in\mathfrak{m}
or
w\in\mathfrak{m}
.
This yields
v^{t}=0
or
w^{t}=0
.
Since
p^{s}\geq t
, we have
v^{p^{s}}=0
or
w^{p^{s}}=0
.
We conclude that either
x=0
or
y=0
, and hence
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
is an integral domain and therefore a field.

The field
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
acts on
\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}
, making it a
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
-linear space, whose basis we denote by
v_{1},\ldots,v_{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\in\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}
.
Let
E_{1},\ldots,E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}
be a basis of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
-linear space.
Then

\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}v_{j}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d_{\mathcal{B}}}\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})\cdot E_{k}v_{j}

as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
-vector space.
Thus,

\Phi^{s}(\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V})=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d_{\mathcal{B}}}\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\cdot\Phi^{s}(E_{k}v_{j}).

We define the basis matrix
C\coloneqq\left(\Phi^{s}(E_{k}v_{j})\right)_{j=1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{V}};k=1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{B}}}
of
\Phi^{s}(\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V})=\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{sn}d}
, treating each
\Phi^{s}(E_{k}v_{j})
as a column vector:

C\coloneqq\left(\Phi^{s}(E_{1}v_{1}),\ldots,\Phi^{s}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}v_{1}),\ldots,\Phi^{s}(E_{1}v_{d_{\mathcal{V}}}),\ldots,\Phi^{s}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}v_{d_{\mathcal{V}}})\right)\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{sn}d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)).

Since
\left\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}\right\}
forms a basis of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
-linear space, taking their
p
-th power gives
\left\{E_{1}^{p},\ldots,E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}\right\}
as a basis of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s+1}}
as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s+1}})
-linear space.
Since
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
is a field,
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s+1}}=\left(\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}\right)^{p}
is a subfield of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
.
Let
F_{1},\ldots,F_{p^{n}}
be a basis of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
as a
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s+1}}
-linear space.
Then,

\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}v_{j}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{p^{n}}\mathcal{B}^{p^{s+1}}\cdot F_{i}v_{j}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{p^{n}}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d_{\mathcal{B}}}\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s+1}})\cdot E_{k}^{p}F_{i}v_{j}

as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s+1}})
-vector space.
So

\Phi^{s+1}(\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V})=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{p^{n}}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d_{\mathcal{B}}}\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(E_{k}^{p}F_{i}v_{j}),

and we define the basis matrix of
\Phi^{s+1}(\mathcal{V}/p\mathcal{V})
:

Q\coloneqq\left(\Phi^{s+1}(E_{1}^{p}F_{1}v_{1}),\ldots,\Phi^{s+1}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}F_{1}v_{d_{\mathcal{V}}}),\ldots,\Phi^{s+1}(E_{1}^{p}F_{p^{n}}v_{1}),\ldots,\Phi^{s+1}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}F_{p^{n}}v_{d_{\mathcal{V}}})\right)\\
\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)).

Let
B
be any element of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
, we will show

Q^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\cdot Q=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C,\ldots,C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big).

Recall that
E_{1},\ldots,E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a basis of
\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
as a
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
-linear space.
For any
k=1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{B}}
, we can write

BE_{k}=b_{1k}^{p^{s}}E_{1}+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}^{p^{s}}E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}},

(3.45)

for some
b_{1k}^{p^{s}},\ldots,b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}^{p^{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{p^{s}})
.
Then

\Phi^{s}(B)\Phi^{s}(E_{k})=b_{1k}\Phi^{s}(E_{1})+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}\Phi^{s}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}),

which yields

\Phi^{s}(B)\Phi^{s}(E_{k}v_{j})=b_{1k}\Phi^{s}(E_{1}v_{j})+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}\Phi^{s}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}v_{j})

for all
j=1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{V}}
.
This means that the matrix
C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C
(that is, the matrix form of linear map
\Phi^{s}(B)
under the new basis
C
) is block diagonal of the form

\displaystyle C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&&&&&&&\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}&&&&&&&\\
&&&b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&&&&\ddots\\
&&&&&&&b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&&&&&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&&&&&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{diag}\left(\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix},\ldots,\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix}}_{d_{\mathcal{V}}\text{ blocks }}\right).

(3.46)

On the other hand, taking power
p
on both sides of Equation (
3.45
) yields

B^{p}E_{k}^{p}=b_{1k}^{p^{s+1}}E_{1}^{p}+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}^{p^{s+1}}E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}.

Hence

\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\Phi^{s+1}(E_{k}^{p})=b_{1k}\Phi^{s+1}(E_{1}^{p})+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}\Phi^{s+1}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}),

which yields

\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\Phi^{s+1}(E_{k}^{p}F_{i}v_{j})=b_{1k}\Phi^{s+1}(E_{1}^{p}F_{i}v_{j})+\cdots+b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}k}\Phi^{s+1}(E_{d_{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}F_{i}v_{j})

for all
i=1,\ldots,p^{n};j=1,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{V}}
.
This means that the matrix
Q^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\cdot Q
(that is, the matrix form of linear map
\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})
under the new basis
Q
) is block diagonal of the form

\displaystyle Q^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\cdot Q

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&&&&&&&\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}&&&&&&&\\
&&&b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&&&&\ddots\\
&&&&&&&b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
&&&&&&&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&&&&&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{diag}\left(\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix},\ldots,\begin{pmatrix}b_{11}&\cdots&b_{1d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}1}&\cdots&b_{d_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathcal{B}}}\\
\end{pmatrix}}_{p^{n}\cdot d_{\mathcal{V}}\text{ blocks }}\right).

(3.47)

Comparing (
3.4
) and (
3.4
), we have

Q^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B^{p})\cdot Q=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C,\ldots,C^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s}(B)\cdot C}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big).

Note that this holds for all
B\in\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
.
Taking
B
as
B_{1}^{p^{s}},\ldots,B_{N}^{p^{s}}\in\mathcal{B}^{p^{s}}
, we can see that for the matrix

R=Q\cdot\operatorname{diag}(\underbrace{C^{-1},\ldots,C^{-1}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}})
, we have

R^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\cdot R=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)

for
i=1,\ldots,N
.
∎

We then strengthen Lemma
3.29
to Proposition
3.28
using a variant of
Hensel lifting
, a common technique in number theory.

Lemma 3.30

(Hensel lifting of the Frobenius splitting)
.

Let
a\geq 1,D\geq 1
.
Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{N}\in\mathsf{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
be pairwise commuting matrices, and let
C_{1},\ldots,C_{N}\in\mathsf{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
be another set of pairwise commuting matrices.
If there exists a matrix
R\in\mathsf{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that

R^{-1}B_{i}R\equiv C_{i}\mod p^{a}

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
,
then there exists effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
\widetilde{R}\in\mathsf{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that

\widetilde{R}^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\widetilde{R}\equiv C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\mod p^{a+1}

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.

Proof.

Since
R^{-1}B_{i}R\equiv C_{i}\mod p^{a}
for all
i
, we can write

C_{i}=R^{-1}B_{i}R+p^{a}M_{i}

for some
M_{i}\in\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
,
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Write
\widetilde{R}=R+p^{a}Q
, and we want to find
\ell
and
Q
such that

(R+p^{a}Q)^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(R+p^{a}Q)\equiv C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\mod p^{a+1}

(3.48)

for all
i
.
Since

(R+p^{a}Q)^{-1}=R^{-1}(I+p^{a}QR^{-1})^{-1}=R^{-1}\Big(I-p^{a}QR^{-1}+p^{2a}(QR^{-1})^{2}-\cdots\Big),

taking modulo
p^{a+1}
yields

(R+p^{a}Q)^{-1}\equiv R^{-1}-p^{a}R^{-1}QR^{-1}\mod p^{a+1}.

Equation (
3.48
) is thus equivalent to

(R^{-1}-p^{a}R^{-1}QR^{-1})B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(R+p^{a}Q)\equiv(R^{-1}B_{i}R+p^{a}M_{i})^{p^{\ell}}\mod p^{a+1},

which can then be rewritten as

R^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R+p^{a}(R^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}Q-R^{-1}QR^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R)\equiv R^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R+p^{a}\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}(R^{-1}B_{i}R)^{k}M_{i}(R^{-1}B_{i}R)^{p^{\ell}-1-k}\\
\mod p^{a+1}.

This is equivalent to

R^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}Q-R^{-1}QR^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}(R^{-1}B_{i}R)^{k}M_{i}(R^{-1}B_{i}R)^{p^{\ell}-1-k}\mod p.

Since
R^{-1}B_{i}R\equiv C_{i}\mod p
, we have
R^{-1}B_{i}^{p^{\ell}}R\equiv C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\mod p
, and the above equation is equivalent to

C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(R^{-1}Q)-(R^{-1}Q)C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}C_{i}^{k}M_{i}C_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1-k}\mod p.

(3.49)

Define the
\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
-linear transformations

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}\colon\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto MC_{i}-C_{i}M,

for
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Here
C_{1},\ldots,C_{N}\in\mathsf{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
are considered as elements in
\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
by taking modulo
p
.

By Lemma
3.22
, we have

\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M)=\sum_{k=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}C_{i}^{k}MC_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1-k},\quad\text{ and }\quad\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(M)=MC_{i}^{p^{\ell}}-C_{i}^{p^{\ell}}M.

Hence, Equation (
3.49
) is equivalent to

-\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(R^{-1}Q)=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{i}),

(3.50)

where we have now taken modulo
p
of the matrices
Q
and
R
.

Recall that for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, the elements
C_{i},C_{j}
commute.
So for all
M\in\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
we have

\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}(M)=MC_{j}C_{i}-C_{j}MC_{i}-C_{i}MC_{j}+C_{i}C_{j}M\\
=MC_{i}C_{j}-C_{j}MC_{i}-C_{i}MC_{j}+C_{j}C_{i}M=\varphi_{j}\varphi_{i}(M).

Therefore
\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}=\varphi_{j}\varphi_{i}
.
Furthermore, since
B_{i},B_{j}
commute, the elements
C_{i}-p^{a}M_{i}=R^{-1}B_{i}R
and
C_{j}-p^{a}M_{j}=R^{-1}B_{j}R
also commute.
Therefore

0\equiv(C_{i}-p^{a}M_{i})(C_{j}-p^{a}M_{j})-(C_{j}-p^{a}M_{j})(C_{i}-p^{a}M_{i})\equiv p^{a}(-M_{i}C_{j}-C_{i}M_{j}+M_{j}C_{i}+C_{j}M_{i})\\
\mod p^{a+1},

so

-\varphi_{j}(M_{i})+\varphi_{i}(M_{j})=-M_{i}C_{j}-C_{i}M_{j}+M_{j}C_{i}+C_{j}M_{i}\equiv 0\mod p.

(3.51)

That is, we have
\varphi_{i}(M_{j})=\varphi_{j}(M_{i})
for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

By Lemma
3.23
, there exist effectively computable
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
\widetilde{Q}\in\mathsf{M}_{D\times D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))

such that
\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(\widetilde{Q})=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{i})
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Let
Q\coloneqq-R\widetilde{Q}
, we have

-\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(R^{-1}Q)=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}}(\widetilde{Q})=\varphi_{i}^{p^{\ell}-1}(M_{i}),

then Equation (
3.50
) (and hence Equation (
3.48
)) is satisfied for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
∎

Combining Lemma
3.29
and
3.30
, we can finally prove Proposition
3.28
:

See
3.28

Proof.

Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{N}
be the image of
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
in
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}/p\mathcal{A}
.
By Lemma
3.29
, there exists
s\geq 0
and
R\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{F}_{p}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that

R^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(B_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\cdot R=\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(B_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big).

(3.52)

Take any
R_{0}\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that
(R_{0}\mod p)=R
. Then Equation (
3.52
) yields

R_{0}^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\cdot R_{0}\equiv\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)\mod p.

(3.53)

Since
A_{1},\ldots,A_{N}
are pairwise commuting matrices, the matrices
\Phi^{s+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+1}},i=1,\ldots,N
pairwise commute, and the matrices
\operatorname{diag}\big(\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}}\big),i=1,\ldots,N
, also pairwise commute.
Therefore we can apply Lemma
3.30
with
a=1
to Equation (
3.53
).
This gives us
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and a matrix
\widetilde{R}_{0}\in\mathsf{GL}_{p^{(s+1)n}d}(\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu))
such that

\widetilde{R}_{0}^{-1}\cdot\left(\Phi^{s+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+1}}\right)^{p^{\ell_{0}}}\cdot\widetilde{R}_{0}\equiv\left(\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}},\ldots,\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)\right)^{p^{\ell_{0}}}\mod p^{2}

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Applying
\Phi^{\ell_{0}}
to the above equation yields

\Phi^{\ell_{0}}(\widetilde{R}_{0})^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}+1}}\cdot\Phi^{\ell_{0}}(\widetilde{R}_{0})\equiv\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}}},\ldots,\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)\mod p^{2}.

Letting
R_{1}\coloneqq\Phi^{\ell_{0}}(\widetilde{R}_{0})
and iterating the above procedure gives us
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ldots,\ell_{e-1}\in\mathbb{N}
as well as matrices
R_{1},R_{2},\ldots,R_{e-1}
, with
R_{a}=\Phi^{\ell_{a-1}}(\widetilde{R}_{a-1})
for each
a=1,2,\ldots,e-1
, such that

R_{a}^{-1}\cdot\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}+1}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}+1}}\cdot R_{a}\\
\equiv\operatorname{diag}\Big(\underbrace{\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}}},\ldots,\Phi^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}}(A_{i})^{p^{s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{a-1}}}}_{p^{n}\text{ blocks}}\Big)\mod p^{a+1}

for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Since we work over the base ring
\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
, we have
A\equiv B\mod p^{e}\iff A=B
.
Therefore, taking
a=e-1
,
R\coloneqq R_{e-1}
and replacing
s+\ell_{0}+\cdots+\ell_{e-1}
by
s
, we obtain Equation (
3.43
).
∎

3.5 Constructing the automaton
\mathscr{U}

Recall that

\Sigma_{p}=\{-(p-1),\ldots,-1,0,1,\ldots,p-1\}
.
In this subsection, we construct an automaton
\mathscr{U}
over the alphabet
\Sigma_{p}^{KN}
, that accepts the solution set to the S-unit equation

A_{1}^{z_{11}}A_{2}^{z_{12}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{1N}}v_{1}+\cdots+A_{1}^{z_{K1}}A_{2}^{z_{K2}}\cdots A_{N}^{z_{KN}}v_{K}=v_{0}

(3.54)

over the
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{V}
.
The idea is similar to what we did in Example
3.3
, but we need to replace the “stabilization” argument
(X^{2}+2X+1)^{2}=X^{4}+2X^{2}+1
, by the “pseudo Frobenius splitting” (
3.43
) of Proposition
3.28
.
First, we explain a few additional conditions that we can suppose without loss of generality.

Additional condition:
R^{-1}\cdot\Phi(A_{i})^{p}\cdot R=\operatorname{diag}(A_{i},\ldots,A_{i})
for all
i=1,\ldots,N
.

Intuitively, this additional condition can be understood as ignoring all the dashed arrows
\dashrightarrow
in the automaton constructed in Example
3.3
(Figure
4
).
This can be done in the same way as in Step 4 of Subsection
3.2
, by replacing each
A_{i}
by
\Phi^{s}(A_{i})^{p^{s}}
and decomposing the solution set of Equation (
3.54
) as a union of solution sets according to their residue modulo
p^{s}
.
Formally, we do the following:

Definition 3.31
.

Let
j\geq 1
be an integer and
r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN}\in\{-(p^{j}-1),\ldots,0,\ldots,p^{j}-1\}
.
For a set
S\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{KN}
, define

\Theta_{j;r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN}}S\coloneqq\{\boldsymbol{z}\in\mathbb{Z}^{KN}\mid\left(p^{j}\cdot\boldsymbol{z}+(r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN})\right)\in S\}.

This is analogous to “truncating” the length-
j
prefix
(r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN})
of a language over
\Sigma_{p}^{KN}
.
When
j=1
, then
r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN}\in\Sigma_{p}^{KN}
, and we write in short
\Theta_{r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN}}
instead of
\Theta_{1;r_{11},\ldots,r_{KN}}
.

Let
s\geq 0
be as in Proposition
3.28
. Taking
\Phi^{s}
on both sides of Equation (
3.54
), it becomes the equation

\sum_{i=1}^{K}\Phi^{s}(A_{1})^{z_{i1}}\Phi^{s}(A_{2})^{z_{i2}}\cdots\Phi^{s}(A_{N})^{z_{iN}}\Phi^{s}(v_{i})=\Phi^{s}(v_{0}),

(3.55)

over the
\Phi^{s}(\mathcal{A})
-module
\Phi^{s}(\mathcal{V})=\mathbb{Z}_{/p^{e}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muX\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)^{p^{sn}d}
.

Let
\mathfrak{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{KN}
